2. Lib.General

Lib.General.LinearAlgebra.Base

Definition 2.1src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/Base.lean: IsVoid
Let $X$ be a set. Then $\IsVoidMACRoBPQ {X}$ is a proposition defined as the proposition that $X$ is empty.
The proposition that α is empty, that is, has no elements; a synonym for `IsEmpty α`.
Theorem 2.2src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/Base.lean: isEmpty_iff_isVoid
Let $X$ be a set. Then $X$ is empty, if and only if $\IsVoidMACRoBPQ {X}$.
Proof. By Lean's tactics, without named lemmas. ∎

Definition 2.5src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/Base.lean: SubmoduleElem
Let $A$ and $M$ be sets. Let $S$ be a submodule of $M$ over $A$. Then $\SubmoduleElemMACRoFFC {S}$ is a set defined as $\SubtypeSet {x_{3}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoDQ {S}{x_{3}})}$.
The type of elements of a submodule S of a module M over a semiring A.

Theorem 2.12src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/Base.lean: Function.Injective.of_ker
Let $F$ be a set. Let $G$ be an additive group. Let $H$ be an additive zero class. Let $f \in F$ such that for all elements $a$ of $G$, if $f (a)$ is equal to $0$, then $a$ is equal to $0$. Then $f$ is injective.
**Alias** of the reverse direction of `injective_iff_map_eq_zero`. --- A homomorphism from an additive group to an additive monoid is injective iff its kernel is trivial. For the iff statement on the triviality of the kernel, see `injective_iff_map_eq_zero'`.
Proof.
By 1 lemma of Mathlib injective_iff_map_eq_zero
. ∎
Theorem 2.13src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/Base.lean: Function.Bijective.of_existsUnique
Let $X$ and $Y$ be sets. Let $f$ be a function from $X$ to $Y$ such that for all elements $b$ of $Y$, $\ExistsUniqueBinder {a}{\EqtMACRoDE {f (a)}{b}}$. Then $f$ is bijective.
**Alias** of the reverse direction of `Function.bijective_iff_existsUnique`.
Proof.
By 1 lemma of Mathlib Function.bijective_iff_existsUnique
. ∎
Theorem 2.14src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/Base.lean: IsCompact.compactSpace
Let $X$ be a topological space. Let $s \in \wp X$ such that $s$ is compact. Then the set of elements of $s$ is a compact space.
**Alias** of the forward direction of `isCompact_iff_compactSpace`.
Proof.
By 1 lemma of Mathlib isCompact_iff_compactSpace
. ∎
Theorem 2.15src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/Base.lean: Continuous.of_at
Let $X$ and $Y$ be topological spaces. Let $f$ be a function from $X$ to $Y$ such that for all elements $x_{2}$ of $X$, $f$ is continuous at $x_{2}$. Then $f$ is continuous.
**Alias** of the reverse direction of `continuous_iff_continuousAt`.
Proof.
By 1 lemma of Mathlib continuous_iff_continuousAt
. ∎
Theorem 2.16src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/Base.lean: Filter.Eventually.all
Let $X$ and $I$ be sets such that $I$ is finite. Let $l$ be a filter on $X$. Let $p$ be a function from elements of $I$ and elements of $X$ to propositions such that for all elements $i_{1}$ of $I$, $\FilterEventually {x_{1}}{p (i_{1} , x_{1})}{l}$. Then $\FilterEventually {x}{\mathrm{forall} (I , \lambda i . p (i , x))}{l}$.
**Alias** of the reverse direction of `Filter.eventually_all`.
Proof.
By 1 lemma of Mathlib Filter.eventually_all
. ∎
Theorem 2.17src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/Base.lean: CharP.of_eq_zero
Let $R$ be a set such that $\mathrm{NonAssocSemiring} (R)$. Let $p \in \Natural$ such that $p$ is prime and $p$ is equal to $0$. Then $p$ is characteristic.
**Alias** of the reverse direction of `CharP.charP_iff_prime_eq_zero`.
Proof.
By 1 lemma of Mathlib CharP.charP_iff_prime_eq_zero
. ∎
Theorem 2.18src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/Base.lean: star_mulVec_dotProduct
Let $A$ be a semiring. Let $m$ and $n$ be finite types. Assume that $A$ is a star ring. Let $B$ be a matrix of $n$ and $A$. Let $x_{4}$ be a function from $n$ to $A$. Let $y$ be a function from $m$ to $A$. Then the sum of the entrywise products of the star of $\mathrm{Matrix\_\_mulVec} (x_{4})$ and $y$ is equal to the sum of the entrywise products of the star of $x_{4}$ and $\mathrm{Matrix\_\_mulVec} (y)$.
Proof.
By 2 lemmas of Mathlib Matrix.dotProduct_mulVec, Matrix.star_mulVec
. ∎
Theorem 2.19src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/Base.lean: diagonal_classical
Let $n$ and $R$ be sets such that $\mathrm{DecidableEq} (n)$. Let $d$ be a function from $n$ to $R$. Then the diagonal of $d$ is equal to the diagonal of $d$.
Proof. By Lean's tactics, without named lemmas. ∎
Theorem 2.20src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/Base.lean: one_classical
Let $n$ and $R$ be sets such that $\mathrm{DecidableEq} (n)$. Then for all elements $x_{2}$ and $x_{3}$ of $n$, $\mathrm{OfNat\_\_ofNat\_\_in} (1 , \mathrm{Matrix\_\_one} , x_{2} , x_{3})$ is equal to $1$.
Proof. By Lean's tactics, without named lemmas. ∎
Theorem 2.21src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/Base.lean: fromBlocks_one'
Let $m$, $n$ and $R$ be sets. Then $\mathrm{Matrix\_\_fromBlocks} (1 , 0 , 0 , 1)$ is equal to $1$.
Proof.
By 2 lemmas of Mathlib Matrix.one_apply, Matrix.ext_iff
. ∎
Theorem 2.22src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/Base.lean: det_classical
Let $m$ be a finite type. Let $R$ be a commutative ring. Let $A$ be a matrix of $m$ and $R$. Assume that $\mathrm{DecidableEq} (m)$. Then the determinant of $A$ is equal to the determinant of $A$.
Proof. By Lean's tactics, without named lemmas. ∎
Theorem 2.23src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/Base.lean: det_fromBlocks_zero₁₂'
Let $m$ and $n$ be finite types. Let $R$ be a commutative ring. Let $A$ and $C$ be matrixes of $m$ and $R$. Let $D$ be a matrix of $n$ and $R$. Then the determinant of $\mathrm{Matrix\_\_fromBlocks} (A , 0 , C , D)$ is equal to $\MatrixdetMACRoGY {A}\times \MatrixdetMACRoGY {D}$.
Proof.
By 1 lemma of Mathlib Matrix.det_fromBlocks_zero₁₂
. ∎

Theorem 2.25src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/Base.lean: det_fromBlocks_one₁₁'
Let $m$ and $n$ be finite types. Let $R$ be a commutative ring. Let $D$ and $B$ be matrixes of $n$ and $R$. Let $C$ be a matrix of $m$ and $R$. Then the determinant of $\mathrm{Matrix\_\_fromBlocks} (1 , B , C , D)$ is equal to the determinant of $D - C \times B$.
Proof.
By 1 lemma of Mathlib Matrix.det_fromBlocks_one₁₁
. ∎
Theorem 2.26src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/Base.lean: det_fromBlocks_one₂₂'

Theorem 2.27src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/Base.lean: charpoly_fromBlocks_zero₂₁'

Theorem 2.28src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/Base.lean: inv_fromBlocks_zero₂₁_of_isUnit_iff'
Let $m$ and $n$ be finite types. Let $R$ be a commutative ring. Let $A$ be a matrix of $m$ and $R$. Let $D$ and $B$ be matrixes of $n$ and $R$ such that $A$ is a unit, if and only if $D$ is a unit. Then the inverse of $\mathrm{Matrix\_\_fromBlocks} (A , B , 0 , D)$ is equal to $\mathrm{Matrix\_\_fromBlocks} (\InvinvMACRoFW {A}, \NegnegMACRoFV {(\InvinvMACRoFW {A}\times B \times \InvinvMACRoFW {D})}, 0 , \InvinvMACRoFW {D})$.
Proof.
By 1 lemma of Mathlib Matrix.inv_fromBlocks_zero₂₁_of_isUnit_iff
. ∎
Theorem 2.29src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/Base.lean: det_kronecker'
Let $m$ and $n$ be finite types. Let $R$ be a commutative ring. Let $A$ be a matrix of $m$ and $R$. Let $D$ be a matrix of $n$ and $R$. Then the determinant of the Kronecker map of $A$ and $D$ is equal to $\MatrixdetMACRoGY {A}^ {\FintypecardMACRoIR {n}}\times \MatrixdetMACRoGY {D}^ {\FintypecardMACRoIR {m}}$.
Proof.
By 1 lemma of Mathlib Matrix.det_kronecker
. ∎
Theorem 2.30src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/Base.lean: twoBlockTriangular_det₀
Let $m$ be a finite type. Let $R$ be a commutative ring. Let $A$ be a matrix of $m$ and $R$. Let $p$ be a function from elements of $m$ to propositions such that for all elements $i$ of $m$, if it is not the case that $p (i)$, then for all elements $j$ of $m$, if $p (j)$, then $A (i , j)$ is equal to $0$. Then the determinant of $A$ is equal to $\MatrixdetMACRoGY {\mathrm{Matrix\_\_toSquareBlockProp} (A , p)}\times \MatrixdetMACRoGY {\mathrm{Matrix\_\_toSquareBlockProp} (A , \lambda x_{3} . \mathrm{not} (p (x_{3})))}$.
Proof.
By 1 lemma of Mathlib Matrix.twoBlockTriangular_det
. ∎

Theorem 2.37src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/Base.lean: adjugate_fin_succ_eq_det_submatrix'

Proof.

Theorem 2.38src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/Base.lean: det_vandermonde_ne_zero_iff'
Let $R$ be a commutative ring. Let $k \in \Natural$. Assume that $R$ is an integral domain. Let $v$ be a function from $\FinMACRoFDI {k}$ to $R$. Then $\MatrixdetMACRoGY {\mathrm{Matrix\_\_vandermonde} (v)}\neq 0$, if and only if $v$ is injective.
Proof.
By 1 lemma of Mathlib Matrix.det_vandermonde_ne_zero_iff
. ∎

Lib.General.Algebra.AdicRingHomExt

Definition 2.39src/Lib/General/Algebra/AdicRingHomExt.lean: frobPow
Let $p , f \in \Natural$. Let $B$ be a set. Assume that $p$ is equal to $0$. Then $\frobPowMACRoDWZ {h_{1}}{f}$ is a ring homomorphism from $B$ to $B$ defined as $\mathrm{RingHom\_\_mk'}$ applied to the power monoid homomorphism of $p ^ {f}$ and $\mathrm{frobPow\_\_\_proof\_1} (h_{1} , f)$.
The ring endomorphism x ↦ x^(p^f) of a commutative ring B in which the prime p is zero.
Theorem 2.40src/Lib/General/Algebra/AdicRingHomExt.lean: frobPow_apply
Let $p , f \in \Natural$. Let $B$ be a commutative ring. Assume that $p$ is prime. Assume that $p$ is equal to $0$. Then $\frobPowMACRoDWZ {h_{1}}{f}$ is equal to the power monoid homomorphism of $p ^ {f}$.
Proof. By frobPow. ∎
Class 2.41src/Lib/General/Algebra/AdicRingHomExt.lean: IsAIC
Let $A$ be a set. Then $\IsAICMACRoBDP {A}$ is a proposition.
A is absolutely integrally closed: every monic polynomial over A of positive degree has a root in A.
Theorem 2.42src/Lib/General/Algebra/AdicRingHomExt.lean: IsAIC.exists_root
Let $A$ be a commutative ring such that $\IsAICMACRoBDP {A}$. Let $P$ be a polynomial over $A$ such that $P$ is monic and $0$ is less than the natural degree of $P$. Then there exists an element $x_{1}$ of $A$, such that $x_{1}$ is a root of $P$.
Proof. By IsAIC. ∎
Instance 2.43src/Lib/General/Algebra/AdicRingHomExt.lean: instIsAICOfIsAlgClosed
instance [IsAlgClosed K] : IsAIC K
Theorem 2.44src/Lib/General/Algebra/AdicRingHomExt.lean: IsAIC.isAlgClosed
Let $K$ be a field such that $\IsAICMACRoBDP {K}$. Then $\mathrm{IsAlgClosed} (K)$.
Proof.
By 2 lemmas of Mathlib Irreducible.natDegree_pos, IsAlgClosed.of_exists_root
. ∎
Theorem 2.45src/Lib/General/Algebra/AdicRingHomExt.lean: IsAIC.exists_eval₂_eq_zero
Let $A$ and $B$ be commutative rings such that $\IsAICMACRoBDP {A}$. Let $P$ be a polynomial over $B$. Let $f \in \RingHomMACRoHO {B}{A}$. Assume that $P$ is monic. Assume that $0$ is less than the natural degree of $P$. Then there exists an element $x_{2}$ of $A$, such that the evaluation of $x_{2}$ and $P$ is equal to $0$.
Proof.
By 2 lemmas of Mathlib Polynomial.eval_map, Polynomial.Monic.map
. ∎
Theorem 2.46src/Lib/General/Algebra/AdicRingHomExt.lean: IsAIC.of_isIntegrallyClosedIn
Let $B$ and $A$ be commutative rings such that $\IsAICMACRoBDP {A}$, $A$ is an algebra over $B$ and $\mathrm{IsIntegrallyClosedIn} (B , A)$. Then $\IsAICMACRoBDP {B}$.
Proof. By IsAIC.exists_eval₂_eq_zero. ∎
Theorem 2.47src/Lib/General/Algebra/AdicRingHomExt.lean: IsAIC.exists_root_of_coeffs_subset_range
Let $A$ and $B$ be commutative rings such that $\IsAICMACRoBDP {A}$. Let $f \in \RingHomMACRoHO {A}{B}$. Let $P$ be a polynomial over $B$ such that $\mathrm{Polynomial\_\_coeffs} (P)$ is a subset of the range of $f$, $P$ is monic and $0$ is less than the natural degree of $P$. Then there exists an element $x_{2}$ of $B$, such that $x_{2}$ is a root of $P$.
Proof. By IsAIC.exists_root
and 3 lemmas of Mathlib Polynomial.lifts_and_natDegree_eq_and_monic, Polynomial.lifts_iff_coeffs_subset_range, Polynomial.IsRoot.map
. ∎
Theorem 2.48src/Lib/General/Algebra/AdicRingHomExt.lean: IsAIC.exists_pow_eq
Let $A$ be a commutative ring such that $\IsAICMACRoBDP {A}$. Let $n$ be a nonzero natural number. Let $a \in A$. Then there exists an element $x_{1}$ of $A$, such that $x_{1} ^ {n}$ is equal to $a$.
Proof.
By 2 lemmas of Mathlib Polynomial.monic_X_pow_sub_C, NeZero.pos
. ∎
Theorem 2.49src/Lib/General/Algebra/AdicRingHomExt.lean: IsAIC.exists_pow_sub_mul_eq
Let $A$ be a commutative ring such that $\IsAICMACRoBDP {A}$. Let $n \in \Natural$ such that $1 < n$. Let $c , b \in A$. Then there exists an element $x_{1}$ of $A$, such that $x_{1} ^ {n}- c \times x_{1}$ is equal to $b$.
In a nonzero absolutely integrally closed ring A, for n > 1 and any c, b in A, the equation x^n − cx = b has a solution x in A.
Proof.
By 6 lemmas of Mathlib LT.lt.le, sub_sub, Polynomial.natDegree_linear_le, Polynomial.IsMonicOfDegree.sub, LE.le.trans_lt, Polynomial.isMonicOfDegree_X_pow
. ∎
Theorem 2.50src/Lib/General/Algebra/AdicRingHomExt.lean: IsAIC.of_surjective
Let $A$ and $B$ be commutative rings such that $\IsAICMACRoBDP {A}$. Let $f$ be a surjective ring homomorphism from $A$ to $B$. Then $\IsAICMACRoBDP {B}$.
Proof. By IsAIC.exists_root_of_coeffs_subset_range. ∎
Theorem 2.51src/Lib/General/Algebra/AdicRingHomExt.lean: IsAIC.of_isLocalization
Let $A$ and $B$ be commutative rings such that $\IsAICMACRoBDP {A}$. Let $S$ be a submonoid of $A$. Assume that $B$ is an algebra over $A$. Assume that $S$ is a localization. Then $\IsAICMACRoBDP {B}$.
If every monic polynomial of positive degree over A has a root in A, the same holds for the localisation B of A at any submonoid S.
Proof. By IsAIC.exists_root_of_coeffs_subset_range
and 7 lemmas of Mathlib IsUnit.mul_right_eq_zero, IsUnit.pow, IsUnit.dvd, IsLocalization.scaleRoots_commonDenom_mem_lifts, IsLocalization.map_units, Polynomial.scaleRoots_eval_mul, Polynomial.lifts_iff_coeffs_subset_range
. ∎
Theorem 2.52src/Lib/General/Algebra/AdicRingHomExt.lean: IsAIC.exists_root_map_eq
Let $A$ and $B$ be commutative rings such that $\IsAICMACRoBDP {A}$. Let $f \in \RingHomMACRoHO {A}{B}$. Assume that $B$ is an integral domain. Let $x_{2} \in B$. Let $P$ be a polynomial over $A$ such that $P$ is monic and the evaluation of $x_{2}$ and $P$ is equal to $0$. Then there exists an element $a$ of $A$, such that $a$ is a root of $P$ and $f (a)$ is equal to $x_{2}$.
Let A be absolutely integrally closed, f: A → B a ring map to a domain, and P a monic polynomial over A. Every root x in B of the image of P is f(a) for some root a of P in A.
Proof.
By 5 lemmas of Mathlib Polynomial.natDegree_mul', Polynomial.leadingCoeff_monic_mul, Polynomial.Monic.def, RingHom.domain_nontrivial, Polynomial.dvd_iff_isRoot
. ∎
Theorem 2.53src/Lib/General/Algebra/AdicRingHomExt.lean: IsAIC.mem_range_of_isIntegralElem
Let $A$ and $B$ be commutative rings such that $\IsAICMACRoBDP {A}$. Let $f \in \RingHomMACRoHO {A}{B}$. Assume that $B$ is an integral domain. Let $x_{2} \in B$ such that $x_{2}$ is an integral element with respect to $f$. Then $x_{2}$ is an element of the range of $f$.
If A is absolutely integrally closed and f: A → B is a ring map to a domain, every element of B integral over A lies in f(A).
Proof. By IsAIC.exists_root_map_eq
and 1 lemma of Mathlib RingHom.mem_range
. ∎
Class 2.54src/Lib/General/Algebra/AdicRingHomExt.lean: ThePrime
Let $p \in \Natural$. Then we can say that $p$ is prime.
The statement that p is prime, as a class that fixes the prime p in scope.

Class 2.56src/Lib/General/Algebra/AdicRingHomExt.lean: PadicField
Let $F$ be a set. Let $p \in \Natural$. Then $\PadicFieldMACRoCGP {F}{p}$ is a proposition.
A normed field F that is a normed algebra over Q_p, not necessarily complete; the prime p is read off from F.

Class 2.58src/Lib/General/Algebra/AdicRingHomExt.lean: PadicIso
Let $x \in \Natural$. Then $\PadicIsoMACRoCGT {x}$ is a proposition.
For a prime ℓ, a choice of ring isomorphism ιℓ from ℂ to the algebraic closure of Q_ℓ.
Definition 2.59src/Lib/General/Algebra/AdicRingHomExt.lean: PadicIso.ιℓ

Theorem 2.67src/Lib/General/Algebra/AdicRingHomExt.lean: pow_dvd_of_map
Let $R$ and $S$ be commutative rings. Let $\pi , x_{1} \in R$. Let $n \in \Natural$. Let $x_{3} \in S$. Let $T$ be a function from $R$ to $S$ such that for all elements $a_{1}$ of $R$, $T (\pi \times a_{1})$ is equal to $x_{3} \times T (a_{1})$. Assume that $x_{3}$ is an element of the non zero divisor of $S$. Assume that for all elements $a$ of $R$, if $x_{3}$ divides $T (a)$, then $\pi$ divides $a$. Assume that $x_{3} ^ {n}$ divides $T (x_{1})$. Then $\pi ^ {n}$ divides $x_{1}$.
Let T be a map between commutative rings R and S with T(πa) = ϖT(a) for all a, ϖ a non-zero-divisor, and ϖ ∣ T(a) implying π ∣ a. Then ϖ^n ∣ T(x) implies π^n ∣ x.
Proof.
By 4 lemmas of Mathlib dvd_def, mul_cancel_left_mem_nonZeroDivisors, mul_assoc, pow_succ'
. ∎

Then $\IsAnalyticFamilyMACRoBEL {f}{p}$.

Theorem 2.103src/Lib/General/Analysis/HolomorphicIntegral.lean: eq_fderiv_of_double

Theorem 2.104src/Lib/General/Analysis/HolomorphicIntegral.lean: norm_sub_le_of_holomorphic_bdd
Let $E$ and $F$ be normed additive commutative groups such that $E$ is a normed space over $\ComplexMACRoAM$ and $F$ is a normed space over $\ComplexMACRoAM$. Let $f$ be a function from $E$ to $F$. Let $r , B \in \Real$ such that for all elements $w$ of $E$, if $\MembershipmemMACRoDQ {\MetricballMACRoHG {c}{r}}{w}$, then $f$ is differentiable at $w$ and $\NormnormMACRoAK {f (w)}\leq B$ and $\MembershipmemMACRoDQ {\MetricballMACRoHG {c}{r}}{z}$. Then $\NormnormMACRoAK {(f (z)- f (c))}\leq \frac{ B + B}{r}\times \NormnormMACRoAK {(z - c)}$.
Schwarz lemma in Lipschitz form: if f is complex differentiable and of norm at most B on the open ball of radius r about c, then ‖f(z) − f(c)‖ ≤ (2B/r)·‖z − c‖ for z in that ball.
Proof.
By 5 lemmas of Mathlib dist_eq_norm, DifferentiableAt.differentiableWithinAt, Complex.dist_le_div_mul_dist_of_mapsTo_ball, norm_sub_le_of_le, Metric.pos_of_mem_ball
. ∎

Lib.General.Algebra.TangentNakayama

Theorem 2.118src/Lib/General/Algebra/TangentNakayama.lean: exists_mem_colon_of_tangent
Let $V$ and $S$ be commutative rings. Let $k$ be a field. Assume that $S$ is an algebra over $V$. Assume that $\mathrm{Algebra\_\_FiniteType} (V , S)$. Assume that $k$ is an algebra over $V$. Let $x_{5}$ be an algebra homomorphism of $S$ and $V$. Let $o$ be an algebra homomorphism of $S$ and $k$ such that the composition of the algebra of $V$ and $k$ and $x_{5}$ is equal to $o$. Let $I$ be an ideal of $S$ such that $I$ is less than or equal to the kernel of $x_{5}$ and for all algebra homomorphisms $w$ of $S$ and the set of dual numbers of $k$, if for all elements $s_{1}$ of $S$, the first component of $w (s_{1})$ is equal to $o (s_{1})$, then if $I$ is less than or equal to the kernel of $w$, then for all elements $s$ of $S$, the second component of $w (s)$ is equal to $0$. Then there exists an element $b$ of $S$, such that $b$ is an element of the colon of $I$ and the kernel of $x_{5}$ and $o (b)\neq 0$.
Nakayama in tangent form: let x : S → V be a section of a finite type V-algebra, φ : V → k a field point, I ⊆ ker x. If every k[ε]-point over φ lifting φ∘x that kills I kills ker x, some b with b·ker x ⊆ I has φ(x(b)) ≠ 0.
Proof.
By 20 lemmas of Mathlib le_rfl, Module.mem_support_iff_of_finite, LinearMap.ext, Equiv.nontrivial_congr, RingHom.mem_ker, map_add, Submodule.annihilator_map_mkQ_eq_colon, exists_ne, algebraMap_smul, Module.nontrivial_dual_iff, Algebra.FinitePresentation.ker_fG_of_surjective, sub_add_sub_comm, Ideal.Quotient.eq_zero_iff_mem, Algebra.FinitePresentation.of_restrict_scalars_finitePresentation, TrivSqZeroExt.ext_iff, IsUnit.mk0, Module.mem_support_iff_nontrivial_residueField_tensorProduct, RingHom.ker_isPrime, Module.Finite.of_fg, Algebra.smul_def
. ∎

Definition 2.122src/Lib/General/Combinatorics/Average.lean: finMean
Let $G$ be a set. Let $f$ be a function from $G$ to $\ComplexMACRoAM$. Then $\finMeanMACRoDUN {f}$ is a complex defined as the quotient of the finite sum of $f$ and the cardinality of $G$.
The average of a complex function f on a finite set G: the sum of its values divided by the cardinality of G.
Definition 2.123src/Lib/General/Combinatorics/Average.lean: classInner
Let $G$ be a set. Let $f$ and $h$ be functions from $G$ to $\ComplexMACRoAM$. Then $\classInnerMACRoDNC {f}{h}$ is a complex defined as $\finMeanMACRoDUN {(\lambda g . \mathrm{starRingEnd\_\_app} (\ComplexMACRoAM , f (g))\times h (g))}$.
The inner product ⟨f, h⟩ of complex functions on a finite set: the average over g of conj f(g) · h(g).

Let $G$ be a set. Let $f$ be a function from $G$ to $\ComplexMACRoAM$. Then $\groupAlgElemMACRoDYD {f}$ is a monoid algebra of $\ComplexMACRoAM$ and $G$ defined as $\mathrm{MonoidAlgebra\_\_ofCoeff} (\EquivsymmappMACRoHL {\mathrm{Finsupp\_\_equivFunOnFinite}}{f})$.

Definition 2.169src/Lib/General/Geometry/ReducedClosure.lean: LineBundle.IsSection

Then $\LineBundleIsSectionMACRoCBP {L}{s}$ is a proposition defined as the proposition that for all elements $i$ of $I$, $\PointFnIsRegularOnMACRoCLQ {s (i)}{\LineBundleopensMACRoCBW {L}{i}}$ and for all elements $i_{1}$ and $j$ of $I$, for all categories of commutative rings $A$, for all homomorphisms $t$ of the spectrum of $A$ and $X$, if $\LandsInMACRoBRB {t}{\LineBundleopensMACRoCBW {L}{i_{1}}}$, then if $\LandsInMACRoBRB {t}{\LineBundleopensMACRoCBW {L}{j}}$, then $s (i_{1} , A , t)$ is equal to $\LineBundletransitionappMACRoFXL {L}{i_{1}}{j}{A}{t}\times s (j , A , t)$.
The family (s_i) is a global section of L: each s_i is regular on U_i, and s_i = g_ij s_j at points landing in U_i and U_j.

Theorem 2.173src/Lib/General/Geometry/ReducedClosure.lean: exists_lift_of_dense_preimage
Let $X$, $Y$ and $Z$ be schemes such that $X$ is reduced. Let $i$ be a homomorphism of $Y$ and $Z$ such that $i$ is a closed immersion. Let $f$ be a homomorphism of $X$ and $Z$ such that the preimage of the range of the homomorphism of the base of $\mathrm{AlgebraicGeometry\_\_LocallyRingedSpace\_\_Hom\_\_toHom} (\mathrm{AlgebraicGeometry\_\_Scheme\_\_Hom\_\_toLRSHom'} (i))$ under the homomorphism of the base of $\mathrm{AlgebraicGeometry\_\_LocallyRingedSpace\_\_Hom\_\_toHom} (\mathrm{AlgebraicGeometry\_\_Scheme\_\_Hom\_\_toLRSHom'} (f))$ is dense. Then there exists a homomorphism $g$ of $X$ and $Y$, such that the composition of $g$ and $i$ is equal to $f$.
Let i: Y → Z be a closed immersion and f: X → Z with X reduced. If the preimage under f of the image of i is dense in X, then f factors through i.
Proof.
By 11 lemmas of Mathlib Set.ext, Eq.le, AlgebraicGeometry.Scheme.IdealSheafData.support_eq_top_iff, AlgebraicGeometry.IsClosedImmersion.lift_fac, GaloisConnection.le_u, TopologicalSpace.Closeds.ext, AlgebraicGeometry.Scheme.Hom.continuous, AlgebraicGeometry.Scheme.Hom.support_ker, AlgebraicGeometry.Scheme.IdealSheafData.map_bot, AlgebraicGeometry.Scheme.IdealSheafData.map_gc, Continuous.closure_preimage_subset
. ∎
Definition 2.174src/Lib/General/Geometry/ReducedClosure.lean: IsDenseFamily
Let $Z$ be a set. Let $X$ be a scheme. Let $\xi$ be a function from elements of $Z$ to homomorphisms of the spectrum of $\mathrm{CommRingCat\_\_of} (\ComplexMACRoAM)$ and $X$. Then $\IsDenseFamilyMACRoBHE {\xi}$ is a proposition defined as the proposition that the function that maps $x$ to $\mathrm{CategoryTheory\_\_ConcreteCategory\_\_hom\_\_app}$ applied to the base of $\mathrm{AlgebraicGeometry\_\_LocallyRingedSpace\_\_Hom\_\_toHom} (\mathrm{AlgebraicGeometry\_\_Scheme\_\_Hom\_\_toLRSHom'} (\xi))$ and the closed point in the prime spectrum of $\ComplexMACRoAM$ is a dense range.
The images of the complex points ξ_z : Spec ℂ → X form a dense subset of X.
Definition 2.175src/Lib/General/Geometry/ReducedClosure.lean: IsSeparatingFamily
Let $Z$ be a set. Let $X$ be a scheme. Let $\xi$ be a function from elements of $Z$ to homomorphisms of the spectrum of $\mathrm{CommRingCat\_\_of} (\ComplexMACRoAM)$ and $X$. Then $\IsSeparatingFamilyMACRoBNA {\xi}$ is a proposition defined as the proposition that $X$ is reduced and $\IsDenseFamilyMACRoBHE {\xi}$.
The family ξ_z : Spec ℂ → X is separating: X is reduced and the images of the ξ_z have dense union.
Theorem 2.176src/Lib/General/Geometry/ReducedClosure.lean: exists_reduced_closure
Let $X$ be a scheme. Let $J$ be a set. Let $\xi$ be a function from elements of $J$ to homomorphisms of the spectrum of $\mathrm{CommRingCat\_\_of} (\ComplexMACRoAM)$ and $X$. Then there exists a scheme $Y$, such that there exists a homomorphism $i$ of $Y$ and $X$, such that there exists a function $e$ from elements of $J$ to homomorphisms of the spectrum of $\mathrm{CommRingCat\_\_of} (\ComplexMACRoAM)$ and $Y$, such that $i$ is a closed immersion, for all elements $z$ of $J$, the composition of $e (z)$ and $i$ is equal to $\xi$ and $\IsSeparatingFamilyMACRoBNA {e}$.
Reduced closure: complex points ξ_z : Spec ℂ → X factor as ξ_z = i ∘ e_z through a closed immersion i : Y → X, with Y reduced and the points e_z dense in Y.
Proof. By exists_lift_of_dense_preimage
and 10 lemmas of Mathlib subset_closure, AlgebraicGeometry.IsReduced.of_openCover, dense_indiscrete, closure_mono, PrimeSpectrum.isRadical_vanishingIdeal, AlgebraicGeometry.Scheme.Hom.comp_apply, AlgebraicGeometry.Scheme.Hom.isEmbedding, Topology.IsInducing.dense_iff, Ideal.isRadical_iff_quotient_reduced, AlgebraicGeometry.affine_isReduced_iff
. ∎

Theorem 2.189src/Lib/General/Geometry/PointFunctions.lean: PointFn.ofSection_appLE
Let $X$ and $Y$ be schemes. Let $U$ be an open of $X$. Let $A$ be a category of commutative rings. Let $x$ be a homomorphism of the spectrum of $A$ and $Y$. Let $p$ be a homomorphism of $Y$ and $X$. Let $W$ be an open of $Y$ such that $W$ is less than or equal to the action of the map of the base of $\mathrm{AlgebraicGeometry\_\_LocallyRingedSpace\_\_Hom\_\_toHom} (\mathrm{AlgebraicGeometry\_\_Scheme\_\_Hom\_\_toLRSHom'} (p))$ and $U$. Definition 2.235src/Lib/General/Geometry/ProjectiveSpace.lean: LineBundle.IsHomogCoords
Let $X$ be a scheme. Let $I$ be a set. Let $A$ be a category of commutative rings. Let $L \in \LineBundleMACRoCBH {X}{I}$. Let $t$ be a homomorphism of the spectrum of $A$ and $X$. Then $\LineBundleIsHomogCoordsMACRoCBK {L}{t}{v}$ is a proposition defined as the proposition that for all elements $j$ of $I$, $\LandsInMACRoBRB {t}{\LineBundleopensMACRoCBW {L}{j}}$, if and only if $v (j)$ is a unit and for all elements $k$ of $I$, if $v (j)$ is a unit, then $\LineBundletransitionappMACRoFXL {L}{j}{k}{A}{t}$ is equal to the product of $v (k)$ and the inverse of $v (j)$.
v gives homogeneous coordinates of the A-valued point t: t lands in U_j exactly when v_j is a unit of A, and then g_jk(t) = v_k/v_j for all k.

Definition 2.248src/Lib/General/Geometry/ProjectiveSpace.lean: LineBundle.IsProjEmbedding
Let $X$ and $S$ be schemes. Let $I$ be a set. Let $L \in \LineBundleMACRoCBH {X}{I}$. Let $s$ be a homomorphism of $X$ and $S$. Then $\LineBundleIsProjEmbeddingMACRoCBN {L}{s}$ is a proposition defined as the proposition that $\LineBundleIsLinearSystemMACRoCBM {L}$, $s$ is proper and for all elements $i$ of $I$, for all categories of commutative rings $A$, for all homomorphisms $a$ and $b$ of the spectrum of $A$ and $X$, if $\LandsInMACRoBRB {a}{\LineBundleopensMACRoCBW {L}{i}}$, $\LandsInMACRoBRB {b}{\LineBundleopensMACRoCBW {L}{i}}$ and for all elements $j$ of $I$, $\LineBundletransitionappMACRoFXL {L}{i}{j}{A}{a}$ is equal to $\LineBundletransitionappMACRoFXL {L}{i}{j}{A}{b}$, then if the composition of $a$ and $s$ is equal to the composition of $b$ and $s$, then $a$ is equal to $b$.
L is a linear system, s: X → S is proper, and morphisms to X with the same composite to S and the same homogeneous coordinates under L are equal.

Class 2.254src/Lib/General/Geometry/ProjectiveSpace.lean: HasProjEmbedding

Theorem 2.255src/Lib/General/Geometry/ProjectiveSpace.lean: hasProjEmbedding_iff

Theorem 2.269src/Lib/General/Geometry/TensorPowers.lean: LineBundle.isProjEmbedding_pi
Let $S$ be a scheme. Let $X$ be a finite type. Let $\nu$ be a function from elements of $X$ to sets. Let $W \in \mathrm{CategoryTheory\_\_Over} (S)$ such that the homomorphism of $W$ is proper. Let $Z$ be a function from $X$ to $\mathrm{CategoryTheory\_\_Over} (S)$. Let $\pi$ be a function from elements $l_{5}$ of $X$ to homomorphisms of $W$ and $Z (l_{5})$. Let $N$ be a function from elements $l_{4}$ of $X$ to elements of $\LineBundleMACRoCBH {\mathrm{CategoryTheory\_\_Over\_\_left} (Z (l_{4}))}{\nu (l_{4})}$ such that for all elements $l_{3}$ of $X$, $\LineBundleIsProjEmbeddingMACRoCBN {N (l_{3})}{\mathrm{CategoryTheory\_\_Over\_\_hom} (Z (l_{3}))}$. Assume that for all elements $T$ of $\mathrm{CategoryTheory\_\_Over} (S)$, for all homomorphisms $x_{1}$ and $y$ of $T$ and $W$, if for all elements $l_{2}$ of $X$, the composition of $x_{1}$ and $\pi (l_{2})$ is equal to the composition of $y$ and $\pi (l_{2})$, then $x_{1}$ is equal to $y$. Then $\LineBundleIsProjEmbeddingMACRoCBN {\LineBundlepiMACRoCBX {(\lambda l_{1} . \LineBundlepullMACRoCCA {N (l_{1})}{\mathrm{CategoryTheory\_\_Over\_\_Hom\_\_left} (\pi (l_{1}))})}{\FinsetunivMACRoDZ}}{\mathrm{CategoryTheory\_\_Over\_\_hom} (W)}$.
Segre: let W be proper over S, with finitely many S-maps π_l: W → Z_l that jointly separate morphisms into W over S. If each N_l satisfies `LineBundle.IsProjEmbedding` on Z_l, so does the Segre product of the π_l^*N_l on W.
Proof. By LineBundle.IsLinearSystem.pull, PointFn.pull, LineBundle.pi, LineBundle.IsLinearSystem.pi, LineBundle.pull
and 2 lemmas of Mathlib CategoryTheory.Over.OverMorphism.ext, Finset.prod_eq_single
. ∎

Theorem 2.272src/Lib/General/Groups/ValuationPair.lean: Finite.int_weight_induction
Let $I$ be a finite set. Let $w$ be a function from $I$ to $\Integer$. Let $P$ be a function from elements of $I$ to propositions such that for all elements $a_{1}$ of $I$, if for all elements $b$ of $I$, if $w (b)< w (a_{1})$, then $P (b)$, then $P (a_{1})$. Let $a \in I$. Then $P (a)$.
Strong induction along an integer weight w on a finite type: if P a follows from P b for all b with w b < w a, then P holds everywhere.
Proof.
By 1 lemma of Mathlib Finite.wellFounded_of_trans_of_irrefl
. ∎
Theorem 2.273src/Lib/General/Groups/ValuationPair.lean: reindex_equiv
Let $F$, $X$ and $Y$ be sets. Let $e \in F$. Then for all functions $p$ from elements of $Y$ to propositions, for all elements $y$ of $Y$, $p (y)$, if and only if for all elements $x_{1}$ of $X$, $p (e (x_{1}))$, for all functions $p_{1}$ from elements of $Y$ to propositions, there exists an element $y_{1}$ of $Y$, such that $p_{1} (y_{1})$, if and only if there exists an element $x_{3}$ of $X$, such that $p_{1} (e (x_{3}))$ and for all elements $x_{5}$ of $\mathrm{Fintype} (X)$, for all elements $x_{6}$ of $\mathrm{Fintype} (Y)$, for all sets $M$, for all functions $f$ from $Y$ to $M$, for all elements $x_{7}$ of $\mathrm{AddCommMonoid} (M)$, $\FinsetSum {\FinsetunivMACRoDZ}{i}{f (i)}$ is equal to $\FinsetSum {\FinsetunivMACRoDZ}{i_{1}}{f (e (i_{1}))}$ and for all elements $x_{8}$ of $\mathrm{CommMonoid} (M)$, $\FinsetProd {\FinsetunivMACRoDZ}{i_{2}}{f (i_{2})}$ is equal to $\FinsetProd {\FinsetunivMACRoDZ}{i_{3}}{f (e (i_{3}))}$.
Change of index along a bijection e: α → β. A universal or existential statement over β, and a finite sum or product over β, may be rewritten over α by substituting e(x).
Proof.
By 5 lemmas of Mathlib Function.Surjective.forall, Function.Bijective.sum_comp, Function.Surjective.exists, Function.Bijective.prod_comp, EquivLike.bijective
. ∎
Theorem 2.274src/Lib/General/Groups/ValuationPair.lean: entrywise

Definition 2.290src/Lib/General/Groups/ValuationPair.lean: inclMatrix

For a map u: m → n of index sets, the n×m matrix whose j-th column is the standard basis vector e_{u(j)}.

The complex diagonal matrix whose diagonal entries are the signs s_i = ±1.

Theorem 2.315src/Lib/General/Groups/ValuationPair.lean: U.coe_inv

Then $\UnitsvalMACRoFDS {\InvinvMACRoFW {g}}$ is equal to the product of $J$, the conjugate transpose of $\UnitsvalMACRoFDS {g}$ and $J$.
Proof. By U.form_mul_inv_eq. ∎

Definition 2.368src/Lib/General/Groups/ValuationPair.lean: IsTameAtLevel

σ^p = 1, p > 0, σ(m) = m, some e has ∑_{i<p} σ^i(e) ≡ 1 mod m, and for 0 < i < p every prime ideal modulo which σ^i is the identity contains m.

Theorem 2.406src/Lib/General/Groups/IrreducibleCharacters.lean: irreducible_criteria_of_isClassFunction

Let f be a class function on a finite group G. (1) f is an irreducible character if ⟨f,f⟩ = 1, Re f(1) ≥ 0 and some class function I has ⟨I,f⟩ ≠ 0 and ⟨χ,f⟩ ∈ ℤ for each character χ with ⟨χ,I⟩ ≠ 0 and χ(1)⟨χ,I(·y)⟩ = ⟨χ,I⟩χ(y) for all y. Theorem 2.407src/Lib/General/Groups/IrreducibleCharacters.lean: IsChar.groupAlgElem_eq_zero

Let $n$, $X$ and $R$ be sets. Let $\mathrm{lev}$ be a function from $n$ to $X$. Then $\unitriangularMACRoEZU {\mathrm{lev}}{R}$ is a subgroup of $\MatrixGeneralLinearGroupMACRoFDH {n}{R}$ defined as $\agreeWithOneOnMACRoDJA {(\lambda x_{3} . \lambda x_{4} . \mathrm{Geq} (\mathrm{lev} (x_{3}), \mathrm{lev} (x_{4})))}{R}{\mathrm{unitriangular\_\_\_proof\_1} (\mathrm{lev})}$.
The group of block upper triangular matrices all of whose diagonal blocks are the identity.
Theorem 2.440src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/ExtendColumns.lean: mem_agreeWithOneOn
Let $R$ be a commutative ring. Let $n$ be a finite type. Let $r$ be a function from elements of $n$ and elements of $n$ to propositions such that for all elements $i$, $k$ and $j$ of $n$, if $r (i , j)$, then $r (i , k)$ or $r (k , j)$. Let $x_{2} \in \MatrixGeneralLinearGroupMACRoFDH {n}{R}$. Then $x_{2}$ is an element of $\agreeWithOneOnMACRoDJA {r}{R}{h}$, if and only if for all elements $a$ and $b$ of $n$, if $r (a , b)$, then $\UnitsvalappMACRoHCH {x_{2}}{a}{b}$ is equal to $1$.
Proof. By Lean's tactics, without named lemmas. ∎
Theorem 2.441src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/ExtendColumns.lean: conj_mem_unitriangular
Let $R$ be a commutative ring. Let $n$ be a finite type. Let $X$ be a linearly ordered set. Let $\mathrm{lev}$ be a function from $n$ to $X$. Let $u , d \in \MatrixGeneralLinearGroupMACRoFDH {n}{R}$ such that $\mathrm{Matrix\_\_BlockTriangular} (\UnitsvalMACRoFDS {d}, \mathrm{lev})$ and $u$ is an element of $\unitriangularMACRoEZU {\mathrm{lev}}{R}$. Then $\InvinvMACRoFW {d}\times u \times d$ is an element of $\unitriangularMACRoEZU {\mathrm{lev}}{R}$.
If d in GL_n(R), R a commutative ring, is block upper triangular for the levels lev and u is block upper triangular with identity diagonal blocks, then d⁻¹ud also has identity diagonal blocks and is block upper triangular.
Proof. By parabolic
and 4 lemmas of Mathlib not_le, Units.inv_mul, Matrix.mul_one, Finset.sum_mul
. ∎
Theorem 2.442src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/ExtendColumns.lean: det_eq_one_of_unitriangular
Let $R$ be a commutative ring. Let $n$ be a finite type. Let $X$ be a linearly ordered set. Let $\mathrm{lev}$ be a function from $n$ to $X$. Let $Z$ be a matrix of $n$ and $R$ such that for all elements $a$ and $b$ of $n$, if $\mathrm{lev} (b)\leq \mathrm{lev} (a)$, then $Z (a , b)$ is equal to $1$. Then the determinant of $Z$ is equal to $1$.
Proof.
By 2 lemmas of Mathlib Matrix.one_apply_ne, Matrix.BlockTriangular.det
. ∎

Theorem 2.454src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/ExtendColumns.lean: exists_GL_mul_inclMatrix

Theorem 2.455src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/AdelicRingDefs.lean: exists_generalized_inverse
Let $k$ be a field. Let $M$ and $N$ be additive commutative groups such that $M$ is a module over $k$ and $N$ is a module over $k$. Let $f$ be a linear map of $M$ and $N$. Then there exists a linear map $s$ of $N$ and $M$, such that for all elements $x_{5}$ of $M$, $f (s (f (x_{5})))$ is equal to $f (x_{5})$.
Proof.
By 2 lemmas of Mathlib LinearMap.exists_rightInverse_of_surjective, LinearMap.exists_extend
. ∎

Theorem 2.457src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/AdelicRingDefs.lean: exists_mul_add_mul_eq

Theorem 2.458src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/AdelicRingDefs.lean: exists_mulVec_eq_of_annihilator
Let $F$ be a field. Let $I$ and $X$ be finite types. Let $P$ and $P'$ be matrixes of $X$ and $F$. Let $s , s' \in \wp X$. Let $X_{1}$ and $Y$ be functions from $X$ to $F$ such that for all functions $l$ from $I$ to $F$, if for all elements $b_{3}$ of $X$, if $b_{3}$ is an element of $s$, then the vector product of $P$ and $b_{3}$ is equal to $0$, then if for all elements $b_{2}$ of $X$, if $b_{2}$ is an element of $s'$, then the vector product of $P'$ and $b_{2}$ is equal to $0$, then the sum of the entrywise products of $\mathrm{Matrix\_\_vecMul} (P)$ and $X_{1}$ is equal to the sum of the entrywise products of $\mathrm{Matrix\_\_vecMul} (P')$ and $Y$. Then there exist functions $x_{3}$ and $y$ from $X$ to $F$, such that $\mathrm{Matrix\_\_mulVec} (x_{3})$ is equal to $\mathrm{Matrix\_\_mulVec} (y)$, for all elements $b$ of $X$, if $b$ is not an element of $s$, then $x_{3} (b)$ is equal to $X_{1} (b)$ and for all elements $b_{1}$ of $X$, if $b_{1}$ is not an element of $s'$, then $y (b_{1})$ is equal to $Y (b_{1})$.
Let s, s' be sets of column indices. If every linear form l killing the columns of P in s and those of P' in s' has l(PX) = l(P'Y), then Px = P'y for some x equal to X off s and y equal to Y off s'.
Proof.
By 8 lemmas of Mathlib Matrix.vecMul_apply, Function.Surjective.forall, Submodule.map_span_le, Submodule.exists_le_ker_of_notMem, Pi.mem_spanSubset_iff, LinearEquiv.surjective, Not.imp_symm, Matrix.dotProduct_mulVec
. ∎

Definition 2.480src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/AdelicRingDefs.lean: preimageLattice
Let $A$, $m$ and $n$ be sets. Let $R'$ be a subring of $A$. Let $M$ be a matrix of $n$ and $A$. Then $\preimageLatticeMACRoEMQ {R'}{M}$ is a submodule of the set of functions from $n$ to $A$ over $\SubtypeSet {x_{2}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoDQ {R'}{x_{2}})}$ defined as the preimage of the scalar restriction of $\SubtypeSet {x_{7}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoDQ {R'}{x_{7}})}$ and $\mathrm{Matrix\_\_mulVecLin} (M)$ and $\intVectorsMACRoEBC {R'}{m}$.
The R-module of vectors v over A such that Mv has entries in R.
Definition 2.481src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/AdelicRingDefs.lean: unitaryLevel
Let $A$ and $n$ be sets. Let $R'$ be a subring of $A$. Let $g$ be a unit of $A$. Let $J$ be a matrix of $n$ and $A$. Then $\unitaryLevelMACRoEZQ {R'}{g}{J}$ is a subgroup of $\SubtypeSet {x_{3}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoDQ {\UMACRoFEQ {J}}{x_{3}})}$ defined as the copy of the subgroup of $\congruenceGLMACRoDOI {(\SubmodulespanMACRoHQ {(\SubtypeSet {x_{4}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoDQ {R'}{x_{4}})})}{\SingletonsingletonMACRoEW {\UnitsvalMACRoFDS {g}}})}{\mathrm{id}}$ and $\UMACRoFEQ {J}$ and $\SubtypeSet {k}{\mathrm{and} (\MembershipmemMACRoDQ {(\intGLMACRoEAZ {n}{R'})}{k}, \IsIntMatrixMACRoBJF {R'}{(\HSMulhSMulMACRoEI {\InvinvMACRoFW {g}}{(\UnitsvalMACRoFDS {k}- 1)})})}$.
The subgroup of the unitary group U(J) over A of elements lying in GL_n(R) and congruent to 1 modulo g.
Definition 2.482src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/AdelicRingDefs.lean: IsUnimodular
Let $A$ and $n$ be sets. Let $R'$ be a subring of $A$. Let $T$ be a matrix of $n$ and $A$. Then $\IsUnimodularMACRoBPK {R'}{T}$ is a proposition defined as the proposition that the conjugate transpose of $T$ is equal to $T$ and there exists a general linear group $u$ of $n$ over $A$, such that $u$ is an element of $\intGLMACRoEAZ {n}{R'}$ and $\UnitsvalMACRoFDS {u}$ is equal to $T$.
The property of a square matrix T over A that it is hermitian and lies in GL_n(R).
Class 2.483src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/AdelicRingDefs.lean: IsHermUnit
Let $A$ and $n$ be sets. Let $J$ be a matrix of $n$ and $A$. Then we can say that $J$ is a hermitian unit.
The matrix J is hermitian and invertible.
Theorem 2.484src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/AdelicRingDefs.lean: IsHermUnit.conjTranspose_eq
Let $A$ be a commutative ring. Let $n$ be a finite type. Assume that $A$ is a star ring. Let $J$ be a matrix of $n$ and $A$ such that $J$ is a hermitian unit. Then the conjugate transpose of $J$ is equal to $J$.
Proof. By IsHermUnit. ∎
Theorem 2.485src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/AdelicRingDefs.lean: IsHermUnit.isUnit
Let $A$ be a commutative ring. Let $n$ be a finite type. Assume that $A$ is a star ring. Let $J$ be a matrix of $n$ and $A$ such that $J$ is a hermitian unit. Then $J$ is a unit.
Proof. By IsHermUnit. ∎

Lib.General.LinearAlgebra.HaarUnique

Definition 2.486src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/HaarUnique.lean: Matrix.IsAccretive
Let $I$ be a set. Let $A$ be a matrix of $I$ and $\ComplexMACRoAM$. Then $\MatrixIsAccretiveMACRoCDD {A}$ is a proposition defined as the proposition that the sum of $A$ and the conjugate transpose of $A$ is a positive part.
The complex matrix A is accretive: its hermitian part is positive definite, A + A* > 0.

Proof.
By 2 lemmas of Mathlib Matrix.PosDef.add, Matrix.PosDef.conjTranspose
. ∎

Lebesgue measure on ℂ^ι divided by π to the power of the number of coordinates.

Theorem 2.492src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/HaarUnique.lean: lintegral_eq_det_mul
Let $I$ be a finite type. Let $u$ be a matrix of $I$ and $\ComplexMACRoAM$ such that $\MatrixdetMACRoGY {u}\neq 0$. Let $g$ be a function from functions from $I$ to $\ComplexMACRoAM$ to elements of $\ENNRealMACRoCG$. Then $\mathrm{MeasureTheory\_\_lintegral}$ applied to the volume and the function that maps $y$ to $g (y)$ is equal to the product of $\mathrm{ENNReal\_\_ofReal} (\NormnormMACRoAK {\MatrixdetMACRoGY {u}}^ {2})$ and $\mathrm{MeasureTheory\_\_lintegral}$ applied to the volume and the function that maps $y_{1}$ to $g$ applied to $\mathrm{Matrix\_\_mulVec} (y_{1})$.
Linear change of variables on ℂ^ι: for a complex square matrix u with det u ≠ 0 and any g : ℂ^ι → [0, ∞], ∫ g(y) dy = |det u|² ∫ g(uy) dy (lower Lebesgue integrals).
Proof. By map_mulVec_haar
and 4 lemmas of Mathlib Continuous.measurableEmbedding, MeasurableEmbedding.lintegral_map, Matrix.mulVec_injective_of_det_ne_zero, ENNReal.mul_inv_cancel_left
. ∎

Theorem 2.496src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/HaarUnique.lean: integral_gauss
Let $I$ be a finite type. Let $A$ be a matrix of $I$ and $\ComplexMACRoAM$ such that $\MatrixIsAccretiveMACRoCDD {A}$. Let $a$ be a function from $I$ to $\ComplexMACRoAM$. Then the Bochner integral of $\realMeasureMACRoEPB {I}$ and the function that maps $x_{3}$ to the exponential of the negation of the sum of the entrywise products of the star of $x_{3}$ and $\mathrm{Matrix\_\_mulVec} (x_{3} - a)$ is equal to the inverse of the determinant of $A$.
Complex Gaussian integral: if A + A* is positive definite, then for every vector a the integral of exp(−x*A(x − a)) over x in ℂ^ι, for Lebesgue measure divided by π^|ι|, equals (det A)⁻¹.
Proof. By map_mulVec_haar, realMeasure, star_mulVec_dotProduct
and 28 lemmas of Mathlib add_mul, Complex.re_eq_add_conj, MeasureTheory.integral_map, Complex.mul_conj', mul_assoc, Matrix.diagonal_smul, smul_smul, Matrix.GeneralLinearGroup.det_ne_zero, IsSelfAdjoint.isHermitian, Matrix.PosDef.isStrictlyPositive, Complex.volume_preserving_equiv_pi, GaussianFourier.integral_cexp_neg_mul_sum_add, Complex.im_eq_sub_conj, Complex.I_sq, CStarAlgebra.isStrictlyPositive_iff_eq_star_mul_self, Matrix.diagonal_add, StarMul.star_mul, MeasureTheory.MeasurePreserving.integral_comp', mul_add, MeasureTheory.integral_fintype_prod_volume_eq_prod, Complex.exp_sum, Finset.sum_neg_distrib, mul_pow, Matrix.IsHermitian.conjStarAlgAut_star_eigenvectorUnitary, Matrix.mulVec_sub, IsSelfAdjoint.conjugate', MeasureTheory.integral_smul_measure, Matrix.mulVec_mulVec

Theorem 2.497src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/HaarUnique.lean: norm_det_le_norm_det_of_posSemidef

Theorem 2.498src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/HaarUnique.lean: norm_det_sq_le_of_posSemidef
Let $I$ be a finite type. Let $A$, $P$, $Q$ and $B$ be matrixes of $I$ and $\ComplexMACRoAM$ such that $\mathrm{Matrix\_\_PosSemidef\_\_in\_4} (\mathrm{Complex\_\_partialOrder} , \mathrm{Matrix\_\_fromBlocks} (P , A , B , Q))$. Then $\NormnormMACRoAK {\MatrixdetMACRoGY {A}}^ {2}\leq \NormnormMACRoAK {\MatrixdetMACRoGY {P}}\times \NormnormMACRoAK {\MatrixdetMACRoGY {Q}}$.
Cauchy–Schwarz inequality for determinants: if the complex block matrix with rows (P, A) and (B, Q), all blocks square of the same size, is positive semidefinite, then |det A|² ≤ |det P|·|det Q|.
Proof. By norm_det_le_norm_det_of_posSemidef
and 12 lemmas of Mathlib Matrix.PosDef.isUnit, Matrix.PosSemidef.mul_mul_conjTranspose_same, Matrix.fromBlocks_mulVec, Matrix.exists_mulVec_eq_zero_iff, Matrix.PosDef.fromBlocks₂₂, Matrix.PosSemidef.posDef_iff_det_ne_zero, Matrix.isHermitian_fromBlocks_iff, Matrix.PosDef.posSemidef, Matrix.PosSemidef.dotProduct_mulVec_zero_iff, mul_comm, Matrix.PosSemidef.submatrix, Matrix.PosDef.inv
. ∎
Theorem 2.499src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/HaarUnique.lean: posSemidef_bessel
Let $I$, $m$ and $n$ be finite types. Let $E$ be a matrix of $n$ and $\ComplexMACRoAM$ such that $\NormnorminMACRoFCP {E}\leq 1$. Let $X$ be a matrix of $I$ and $\ComplexMACRoAM$. Then $\mathrm{Matrix\_\_PosSemidef\_\_in\_4}$ holds for $\mathrm{Complex\_\_partialOrder}$ and the product of $X$, the difference between $1$ and the product of $E$ and the conjugate transpose of $E$ and the conjugate transpose of $X$.
Bessel's inequality for complex matrices: if E has operator norm at most 1, then X(1 − EEᴴ)Xᴴ is positive semidefinite for every X.
Proof.
By 6 lemmas of Mathlib Matrix.posSemidef_self_mul_conjTranspose, Matrix.PosSemidef.mul_mul_conjTranspose_same, Matrix.PosSemidef.nonneg, Matrix.l2_opNorm_conjTranspose, CStarAlgebra.norm_le_one_iff_of_nonneg, Matrix.l2_opNorm_mul
. ∎
Theorem 2.500src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/HaarUnique.lean: measurePreserving_sigma
Let $I$ and $Y$ be finite types. Let $X$ be a measure space. Let $n$ be a function from elements of $I$ to sets such that for all elements $i_{3}$ of $I$, $n (i_{3})$ is a finite type. For a bijection e from the disjoint union of finite sets n_i onto Y, the regrouping map α^Y → ∏_i α^(n_i), x ↦ (x(e(i,j)))_{i,j}, preserves the product volume measures.
Proof.
By 9 lemmas of Mathlib MeasureTheory.volume_preserving_arrowCongr', MeasurableEquiv.map_apply, MeasureTheory.volume_pi_pi, MeasureTheory.MeasurePreserving.symm, Fintype.prod_sigma, MeasureTheory.Measure.pi_eq, Sigma.forall, MeasureTheory.MeasurePreserving.comp, MeasureTheory.MeasurePreserving.id
. ∎

Theorem 2.503src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/HaarUnique.lean: addHaar_eq_of_integral_eq_one
Let $G$ be an additive group such that $G$ is a topological space, $G$ is a topological additive group, $G$ is a locally compact space, $G$ is a second countable topology, $G$ is a measurable space and $G$ is a Borel space. Let $\mu$ and $\nu$ be measures of $G$ such that $\mu$ is an additive Haar measure and $\nu$ is an additive Haar measure. Let $f$ be a function from $G$ to $\Real$ such that the Bochner integral of $\nu$ and $f$ is equal to $1$ and the Bochner integral of $\mu$ and $f$ is equal to $1$. Then $\mu$ is equal to $\nu$.
Two Haar measures μ, ν on a second countable locally compact additive group are equal if some real function f has integral 1 for both.
Proof.
By 1 lemma of Mathlib MeasureTheory.Measure.isAddLeftInvariant_eq_smul
. ∎

Class 2.514src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/HuaIntegral.lean: HasGaussianIntegral
Let $V$ be a set. Let $a \in \Real$. Let $q$ be a function from $V$ to $\Real$. Then $\HasGaussianIntegralMACRoAXL {a}{q}$ is a proposition.
The volume on V is Gaussian-normalised for the real function q with exponent a: ∫ exp(−t q(x)) dx = (π/t)^a for all t > 0.

Proof. By Lean's tactics, without named lemmas. ∎
Theorem 2.516src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/HuaIntegral.lean: lintegral_inv_add_rpow
Let $V$ be a measure space. Let $q$ be a function from $V$ to $\Real$. Let $a , s , c \in \Real$. Assume that $\mathrm{MeasureTheory\_\_SigmaFinite}$ holds for the volume. Assume that $\HasGaussianIntegralMACRoAXL {a}{q}$. Assume that $0 < c$. Assume that $a < s$. Assume that for all elements $x_{2}$ of $V$, $0 \leq q (x_{2})$. Assume that $0 < s$. Assume that $\mathrm{autoParam}$ holds for the proposition that $q$ is measurable and $\lintegralinvaddrpowautoMACRoEEQ$. Then $\mathrm{MeasureTheory\_\_lintegral}$ applied to the volume and the function that maps $x_{1}$ to $\mathrm{ENNReal\_\_ofReal} (\InvinvMACRoFW {(c + q (x_{1}))}^ {s})$ is equal to $\mathrm{ENNReal\_\_ofReal}$ applied to the product of the quotient of $\RealpiMACRoAN ^ {a}$ and the Gamma of $s$, $(\frac{ 1}{c})^ {(s - a)}$ and the Gamma of $s - a$.

Definition 2.549src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/PartialDerivComm.lean: heckeFun
Let $A$, $G$ and $W$ be sets. Let $\pi$ be a representation of $G$ and $W$. Let $K$ be a subgroup of $G$. Let $f$ be a function from $G$ to $A$. Then $\heckeFunMACRoDYO {\pi}{K}{f}$ is an end of $A$ and $W$ defined as the finite sum of the function that maps $q$ to $\HSMulhSMulMACRoEI {f (\mathrm{Quotient\_\_out} (q))}{\pi (\mathrm{Quotient\_\_out} (q))}$.
For a function f on G, the operator ∑ f(g)π(g), summed over chosen representatives g of G/K (zero if infinitely many terms are nonzero): the action of f for vol K = 1.
Definition 2.550src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/PartialDerivComm.lean: heckeCoset
Let $A$, $G$ and $W$ be sets. Let $\pi$ be a representation of $G$ and $W$. Let $K$ be a subgroup of $G$. Let $g \in G$. Then $\heckeCosetMACRoDYM {\pi}{K}{g}$ is an end of $A$ and $W$ defined as $\heckeFunMACRoDYO {\pi}{K}{\mathrm{Set\_\_indicator} (\mathrm{DoubleCoset\_\_doubleCoset} (g , K , K), 1)}$.
The Hecke operator of the double coset KgK: the sum of π(h) over chosen representatives h of the cosets hK in KgK.
Definition 2.551src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/PartialDerivComm.lean: classAut
Let $G$ be a set. Let $\Gamma$ and $K'$ be subgroups of $G$. Let $g \in G$. Then $\classAutMACRoDNB {\Gamma}{K'}{g}$ is a subgroup of $G$ defined as the minimum of $\Gamma$ and the preimage of the monoid homomorphism of $\mathrm{MulAut\_\_conj\_\_app} (\InvinvMACRoFW {g})$ and $K'$.
The subgroup Γ ∩ gKg⁻¹, the stabiliser in Γ of the coset gK.
Definition 2.552src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/PartialDerivComm.lean: DoubleCosets
Let $G$ be a set. Let $\Gamma$ and $K'$ be subgroups of $G$. Then $\DoubleCosetsMACRoAJC {\Gamma}{K'}$ is a set defined as the set of quotients of $\Gamma$ and $K'$.
The set Γ\G/K of double cosets of a group G with respect to subgroups Γ and K.
Theorem 2.553src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/PartialDerivComm.lean: mem_levelSpace
Let $A$ be a commutative semiring. Let $G$ be a group. Let $W$ be an additive commutative monoid such that $W$ is a module over $A$. Let $K$ be a subgroup of $G$. Let $\pi$ be a representation of $G$ and $W$. Let $\chi \in \MonoidHomMACRoHP {(\SubtypeSet {x_{6}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoDQ {K}{x_{6}})})}{A}$. Let $v \in W$. Then $v$ is an element of $\levelSpaceMACRoEDW {\pi}{K}{\chi}$, if and only if for all elements $k$ of $\SubtypeSet {x_{5}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoDQ {K}{x_{5}})}$, $\DFunLikecoeappMACRoHM {\pi}{k}$ is equal to $\HSMulhSMulMACRoEI {\chi (k)}{v}$.
Proof. By Lean's tactics, without named lemmas. ∎
Theorem 2.554src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/PartialDerivComm.lean: map_mem_levelSpace_of_commute
Let $A$ be a commutative semiring. Let $G$ be a group. Let $W$ be an additive commutative monoid such that $W$ is a module over $A$. Let $K$ be a subgroup of $G$. Theorem 2.555src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/PartialDerivComm.lean: mem_classAut
Let $G$ be a group. Let $K$ and $H$ be subgroups of $G$. Let $g , \gamma \in G$. Then $\gamma$ is an element of $\classAutMACRoDNB {H}{K}{g}$, if and only if $\gamma$ is an element of $H$ and $\InvinvMACRoFW {g}\times \gamma \times g$ is an element of $K$.
Proof. By classAut. ∎
Theorem 2.556src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/PartialDerivComm.lean: exists_eq_map_mul_rep_mul
Let $G$ and $X$ be groups. Let $e \in \MonoidHomMACRoHP {X}{G}$. Let $K$ be a subgroup of $G$. Let $r$ be a function from quotients of the range of $e$ and $K$ to elements of $G$ such that for all quotients $c_{1}$ of the range of $e$ and $K$, $\mathrm{DoubleCoset\_\_mk}$ applied to the range of $e$, $K$ and $r (c_{1})$ is equal to $c_{1}$. Let $g \in G$. Then there exists a quotient $c$ of the range of $e$ and $K$, such that there exists an element $\gamma$ of $X$, such that there exists an element $k$ of $\SubtypeSet {x_{3}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoDQ {K}{x_{3}})}$, such that $g$ is equal to $e (\gamma)\times r (c)\times k$.
Let e : Δ → G be a homomorphism and r a system of representatives of e(Δ)\G/K. Then every g in G is e(γ) r(c) k for some class c, γ ∈ Δ, k ∈ K.
Proof.
By 1 lemma of Mathlib DoubleCoset.eq
. ∎
Theorem 2.557src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/PartialDerivComm.lean: eq_and_eq_one_of_doubleCoset_rep
Let $G$ and $X$ be groups. Let $K$ be a subgroup of $G$. Let $e \in \MonoidHomMACRoHP {X}{G}$. Let $r$ be a function from quotients of the range of $e$ and $K$ to elements of $G$ such that for all quotients $c_{1}$ of the range of $e$ and $K$, $\mathrm{DoubleCoset\_\_mk}$ applied to the range of $e$, $K$ and $r (c_{1})$ is equal to $c_{1}$. Let $c$ and $d$ be quotients of the range of $e$ and $K$ such that for all elements $\gamma_{1}$ of $X$, if $\InvinvMACRoFW {r (d)}\times e (\gamma_{1})\times r (d)$ is an element of $K$, then $\gamma_{1}$ is equal to $1$. Let $\gamma \in X$ such that $\InvinvMACRoFW {r (c)}\times e (\gamma)\times r (d)$ is an element of $K$. Then $c$ is equal to $d$ and $\gamma$ is equal to $1$.
Let e : Δ → G be a homomorphism and r a system of representatives of e(Δ)\G/K, and let Δ act freely at the point r(d)K of G/K. Then e(γ) r(d)K = r(c)K only for c = d and γ = 1.
Proof.
By 2 lemmas of Mathlib InvMemClass.inv_mem, DoubleCoset.eq
. ∎
Definition 2.558src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/PartialDerivComm.lean: classSum
Let $G$ and $W$ be sets. Let $\Gamma$ and $K$ be subgroups of $G$. Let $f$ be a function from $G$ to $W$. Then $\classSumMACRoDNG {\Gamma}{K}{f}$ is an element of $W$ defined as the finite sum of the function that maps $c$ to $\HSMulhSMulMACRoEI {\InvinvMACRoFW {\mathrm{Nat\_\_card} (\SubtypeSet {x_{3}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoDQ {(\classAutMACRoDNB {\Gamma}{K}{\mathrm{Quotient\_\_out} (c)})}{x_{3}})})}}{f (\mathrm{Quotient\_\_out} (c))}$.
The class sum of f: the sum over the double cosets c in Γ\G/K of f(g_c)/#(Γ ∩ g_c K g_c⁻¹), g_c a chosen representative.
Theorem 2.559src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/PartialDerivComm.lean: isLocallyConstant_of_eventually_mul
Let $G$ be a group such that $G$ is a topological space and $G$ is a topological group. Let $X$ be a set. Let $f$ be a function from $G$ to $X$ such that $\FilterEventually {k}{\mathrm{forall} (G , \lambda x_{2} . \EqtMACRoDE {f (x_{2} \times k)}{f (x_{2})})}{\nhdsMACRoGQ {1}}$. Then $f$ is locally constant.
A function on a topological group that is invariant under right translation by all elements of a neighbourhood of 1 is locally constant.
Proof.
By 3 lemmas of Mathlib IsLocallyConstant.iff_eventually_eq, Filter.Eventually.mono, map_mul_left_nhds_one
. ∎

Theorem 2.564src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/PartialDerivComm.lean: relIndex_mul_measure_inf
Let $G$ be a group. Let $K$ and $H$ be subgroups of $G$. Assume that $G$ is a topological space. Assume that $G$ is a topological group. Assume that $K$ is compact. Assume that $H$ is open. Assume that $G$ is a measurable space. Assume that $G$ is a Borel space. Assume that $G$ is a space. Let $\mu$ be a measure of $G$ such that $\mu$ is a multiplicative left invariant. Then the product of the relative index of $H$ and $K$ and $\mu$ is equal to $\mu$.
For a left invariant Borel measure μ on a Hausdorff topological group, a compact subgroup K and an open subgroup H, one has [K : H ∩ K]·μ(H ∩ K) = μ(K).
Proof. By IsCompact.relIndex_ne_zero
and 6 lemmas of Mathlib IsOpen.measurableSet, MeasurableEmbedding.comap_apply, MeasureTheory.Subgroup.index_mul_measure, IsCompact.measurableSet, MeasurableEmbedding.subtype_coe, MeasureTheory.Measure.IsMulLeftInvariant.comap
. ∎

Theorem 2.568src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/PartialDerivComm.lean: hasDerivAt_det

Lib.General.LinearAlgebra.SubmersionZero

Theorem 2.570src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/SubmersionZero.lean: tensor_eq_zero_of_separating
Let $U$ and $V$ be additive commutative groups. Let $R$ be a commutative ring. Let $I$ be a set. Assume that $R$ is an integral domain. Assume that $R$ is a principal ideal ring. Assume that $U$ is a module over $R$. Assume that $V$ is a module over $R$. Assume that $\mathrm{Module\_\_IsTorsionFree} (R , V)$. Let $f$ be a function from elements of $I$ to duals of $R$ and $U$ such that for all elements $a$ of $U$, if for all elements $i_{1}$ of $I$, $f (i_{1} , a)$ is equal to $0$, then $a$ is equal to $0$. Let $Z$ be a tensor product of $U$ and $V$ such that for all elements $i$ of $I$, $\mathrm{TensorProduct\_\_lift\_\_app}$ applied to the composition of $\mathrm{LinearMap\_\_lsmul} (R , V)$ and $f (i)$ and $Z$ is equal to $0$. Then $Z$ is equal to $0$.
Over a principal ideal domain R, with V torsion-free: if linear forms f_i on U have common kernel 0, so do the maps f_i ⊗ 1 : U ⊗ V → V.
Proof.
By 6 lemmas of Mathlib Finsupp.ext, Finsupp.sum_fintype, TensorProduct.exists_finite_submodule_right_of_setFinite, Module.Basis.linearIndependent, LinearIndependent.eq_coords_of_eq, TensorProduct.eq_repr_basis_right
. ∎
Theorem 2.571src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/SubmersionZero.lean: norm_mulVec_le
Let $K$ be a normed field. Let $l$ and $m$ be finite types. Assume that $K$ is a ultrametric distance. Let $M$ be a matrix of $m$ and $K$. Let $c \in \Real$ such that for all elements $i$ of $l$, for all elements $j$ of $m$, $\NormnormMACRoAK {M (i , j)}\leq c$. Let $x_{3}$ be a function from $m$ to $K$. Assume that $\mathrm{autoParam} (\mathrm{Leq} (0 , c), \normmulVecleautoMACRoEIN)$. Then the norm of $\mathrm{Matrix\_\_mulVec} (x_{3})$ is less than or equal to $c \times \NormnormMACRoAK {x_{3}}$.
Over an ultrametric normed field, if c ≥ 0 bounds the absolute values of all entries of a matrix M, then ‖Mx‖ ≤ c‖x‖ in the sup norm for every vector x.
Proof.
By 5 lemmas of Mathlib mul_nonneg, pi_norm_le_iff_of_nonneg, norm_le_pi_norm, mul_le_mul, IsUltrametricDist.norm_sum_le_of_forall_le_of_nonneg
. ∎
Theorem 2.572src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/SubmersionZero.lean: eq_zero_of_submersion
Let $E$ and $F$ be normed additive commutative groups such that $E$ is a normed space over $\ComplexMACRoAM$, $E$ is a complete space, $F$ is a normed space over $\ComplexMACRoAM$ and $F$ is a complete space. Let $G$ be a function from $F$ to $\ComplexMACRoAM$. Let $f$ be a function from $E$ to $F$. Let $f'$ be a continuous linear map of $E$ and $F$. Let $a \in E$. Assume that $\mathrm{AnalyticOnNhd} (\ComplexMACRoAM , G , \SetunivMACRoFT)$. Assume that $\mathrm{HasStrictFDerivAt} (f , f' , a)$. Assume that the range of $\mathrm{ContinuousLinearMap\_\_toLinearMap} (f')$ is equal to the top. Assume that $\FilterEventually {x_{4}}{\EqtMACRoDE {G (f (x_{4}))}{0}}{\nhdsMACRoGQ {a}}$. Let $y \in F$. Then $G (y)$ is equal to $0$.
Let G be entire on a complex Banach space F, and f: E → F strictly differentiable at a with surjective derivative. If G∘f vanishes near a, then G = 0.
Proof.
By 4 lemmas of Mathlib Eq.ge, AnalyticOnNhd.eq_of_eventuallyEq, HasStrictFDerivAt.map_nhds_eq_of_surj, analyticOnNhd_const
. ∎

Theorem 2.573src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/CommonEigenvectors.lean: exists_auerbach
Let $K$ be a field. Let $U$ be an additive commutative group. Let $A$ be a topological space. Let $L$ be a normed field. Let $I$ be a finite type. Assume that $U$ is a module over $K$. Assume that $\mathrm{Module\_\_Finite} (K , U)$. Assume that $A$ is a compact space. Let $b$ be a basis of $U$ over $K$. Let $x_{6}$ be a function from elements of $A$ to duals of $K$ and $U$ such that for all elements $v_{1}$ of $U$, if for all elements $a_{2}$ of $A$, $x_{6} (a_{2} , v_{1})$ is equal to $0$, then $v_{1}$ is equal to $0$. Let $\phi \in \RingHomMACRoHO {K}{L}$ such that for all elements $v$ of $U$, the function that maps $a_{1}$ to $\phi (x_{6} (a_{1} , v))$ is continuous. Then there exists a function $S$ from $I$ to $A$, such that there exists a basis $z$ of $U$ over $K$, such that for all elements $k$ of $I$, the coordinate of $z$ and $k$ is equal to $x_{6} (S (k))$ and for all elements $a$ of $A$, for all elements $k_{1}$ of $I$, $\NormnormMACRoAK {\phi (x_{6} (a , z (k_{1})))}\leq 1$.
Auerbach's lemma. Let linear forms ℓ_a on a finite-dimensional K-space, a in a compact space, have only 0 as common zero, with φ∘ℓ_a(v) continuous in a for φ: K → L normed. Then some ℓ_a are the coordinates of a basis z with all ‖φ(ℓ_a(z_k))‖ ≤ 1.
Proof.
By 18 lemmas of Mathlib Continuous.comp', MultilinearMap.ext_of_span_eq_top, norm_pos_iff, Eq.ge, Continuous.exists_forall_ge', Function.comp_update, continuous_matrix, Continuous.matrix_det, Eq.trans_le, Continuous.norm, RingHom.map_det, AlternatingMap.coe_multilinearMap_injective, LT.lt.trans_le, continuous_apply, Submodule.span_eq_top_of_ne_zero, Module.Basis.det_ne_zero, Module.Basis.det_smul_mk_coord_eq_det_update, Module.Basis.is_basis_iff_det
. ∎

Definition 2.576src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/CommonEigenvectors.lean: IsTraceNondeg
Let $k$, $G$ and $n$ be sets. Let $r \in \MonoidHomMACRoHP {G}{(\MatrixGeneralLinearGroupMACRoFDH {n}{k})}$. Then $\IsTraceNondegMACRoBPD {r}$ is a proposition defined as the proposition that for all matrixes $x_{3}$ of $n$ and $k$, if $x_{3}$ is an element of the span of the range of the function that maps $x_{4}$ to $\UnitsvalMACRoFDS {r (x_{4})}$, then if for all elements $g$ of $G$, the trace of $x_{3} \times \UnitsvalMACRoFDS {r (g)}$ is equal to $0$, then $x_{3}$ is equal to $0$.
Non-degeneracy of the trace form on the span of r(G): any x in that span with tr(x r(g)) = 0 for all g is zero.
Definition 2.577src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/CommonEigenvectors.lean: FreeInts
Let $n \in \Natural$. Let $e$ be a function from $\FinMACRoFDI {n}$ to $\Integer$. Then $\FreeIntsMACRoAQW {e}$ is a proposition defined as the proposition that for all $d \leq (n + 1)^ {2}$ in $\Natural$, $\mathrm{Finsupp\_\_weight} (e)$ is injective on $\SubtypeSet {\alpha}{\mathrm{Eq} (\mathrm{Finsupp\_\_degree\_\_app} (\alpha), d)}$.
The integers e_i are free: vectors α, β of natural numbers with Σα_i = Σβ_i ≤ (n+1)² and Σα_i e_i = Σβ_i e_i are equal.
Theorem 2.578src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/CommonEigenvectors.lean: minors_eq_zero_iff

For functions v_i: X → k to a field k, indexed by a finite set n, the determinants det(v_j(x_i)) vanish for all families (x_i) of points of X indexed by n if and only if the v_i are linearly dependent over k.
Proof.
By 7 lemmas of Mathlib MultilinearMap.ext_of_span_eq_top, LinearIndependent.of_comp, Matrix.det_eq_zero_of_not_linearIndependent_cols, Eq.trans_ne, span_flip_eq_top_iff_linearIndependent, Matrix.det_one, one_ne_zero
. ∎
Theorem 2.579src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/CommonEigenvectors.lean: exists_homogenization
Let $I$ be a set. Let $R$ be a commutative ring. Let $N \in \Natural$. Let $F$ and $P$ be multivariate polynomials of $I$ and $R$ such that $\mathrm{MvPolynomial\_\_IsHomogeneous} (F , 1)$ and the total degree of $P$ is less than or equal to $N$. Homogenisation by a linear form F: for a polynomial P over R of total degree at most N there is a homogeneous G of degree N with φ(G) = φ(P) for every R-algebra map φ with φ(F) = 1.
Proof.
By 5 lemmas of Mathlib MvPolynomial.homogeneousComponent_mem, MvPolynomial.sum_homogeneousComponent, MvPolynomial.IsHomogeneous.pow, MvPolynomial.IsHomogeneous.mul, MvPolynomial.IsHomogeneous.sum
. ∎

Definition 2.604src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/SmoothMatrices.lean: IsSampleTotal

a = ∑ w_i F(p_i) for finitely many weighted points p_i, and ∑ w_i #M(p_i, y) = c for every y such that F(x) ≠ 0 whenever M(x, y) is nonempty.
Theorem 2.605src/Lib/General/LinearAlgebra/SmoothMatrices.lean: IsSampleTotal.mul_eq_of_double_count
Let $P$, $Q$, $A$ and $B$ be sets. Let $c , d , a , b \in \ComplexMACRoAM$. Let $M \in P \rightarrow P \rightarrow \wp A$. Let $N \in Q \rightarrow Q \rightarrow \wp B$. Let $F$ be a function from $P$ to $\ComplexMACRoAM$. Let $F'$ be a function from $Q$ to $\ComplexMACRoAM$. Assume that $\IsSampleTotalMACRoBMV {M}{F}{c}{a}$. Then $\EqtMACRoDE {b \times c}{d \times a}$.

Definition 2.626src/Lib/General/Measure/SubgroupIntegral.lean: Subgroup.Cocpt
Let $G$ be a set. Let $N$ be a subgroup of $G$. Then $\SubgroupCocptMACRoCZQ {N}$ is a proposition defined as the proposition that there exists an element $C$ of $\wp G$, such that $C$ is compact and for all elements $g$ of $G$, there exists an element $\gamma$ of $G$, such that $\gamma$ is an element of $N$ and $\gamma \times g$ is an element of $C$.
The subgroup N of G is cocompact: there is a compact C ⊂ G such that every g has a translate γg in C with γ ∈ N.
Definition 2.627src/Lib/General/Measure/SubgroupIntegral.lean: AddSubgroup.Cocpt
Let $G$ be a set. Let $N$ be a subgroup of $G$. Then $\AddSubgroupCocptMACRoKC {N}$ is a proposition defined as the proposition that there exists an element $C$ of $\wp G$, such that $C$ is compact and for all elements $g$ of $G$, there exists an element $\gamma$ of $G$, such that $\gamma$ is an element of $N$ and $\gamma + g$ is an element of $C$.
Definition 2.628src/Lib/General/Measure/SubgroupIntegral.lean: Subgroup.FixesLeft
Let $G$ and $W$ be sets. Let $N$ be a subgroup of $G$. Let $F$ be a function from $G$ to $W$. Then $\SubgroupFixesLeftMACRoCZR {N}{F}$ is a proposition defined as the proposition that for all elements $\gamma$ of $G$, if $\gamma$ is an element of $N$, then for all elements $g$ of $G$, $F (\gamma \times g)$ is equal to $F (g)$.
The function F on G is left invariant under the subgroup N: F(γg) = F(g) for all γ ∈ N and g ∈ G.
Definition 2.629src/Lib/General/Measure/SubgroupIntegral.lean: IsCutoff
Let $H$ and $X$ be sets. Let $\nu$ be a measure of $H$. Let $\beta$ be a function from $X$ to $\ENNRealMACRoCG$. Then $\IsCutoffMACRoBHC {\nu}{\beta}$ is a proposition defined as the proposition that $\beta$ is measurable and for all elements $x_{3}$ of $X$, $\mathrm{MeasureTheory\_\_lintegral} (\nu , \lambda h . \beta (\HSMulhSMulMACRoEI {h}{x_{3}}))$ is equal to $1$.
The property that β: X → [0, ∞] is measurable and ∫_H β(hx) dν(h) = 1 for every x in X.
Definition 2.630src/Lib/General/Measure/SubgroupIntegral.lean: IsAddCutoff
Let $H$ and $X$ be sets. Let $\nu$ be a measure of $H$. Let $\beta$ be a function from $X$ to $\ENNRealMACRoCG$. Then $\IsAddCutoffMACRoBDZ {\nu}{\beta}$ is a proposition defined as the proposition that $\beta$ is measurable and for all elements $x_{3}$ of $X$, $\mathrm{MeasureTheory\_\_lintegral} (\nu , \lambda h . \beta (\HVAddhVAddMACRoB {h}{x_{3}}))$ is equal to $1$.
Definition 2.631src/Lib/General/Measure/SubgroupIntegral.lean: orbitMeasure
Let $H$ and $X$ be sets. Let $\nu$ be a measure of $H$. Let $\mu$ be a measure of $X$. Then $\orbitMeasureMACRoEJO {\nu}{\mu}$ is a measure of $X$ defined as $\mathrm{MeasureTheory\_\_Measure\_\_withDensity} (\mu , \mathrm{Classical\_\_epsilon} (\IsCutoffpartMACRoFTH {\nu}))$.
The measure βμ on X for a chosen cutoff β of the action of (H, ν); integrating an H-invariant function against it gives the integral over H\X.
Definition 2.632src/Lib/General/Measure/SubgroupIntegral.lean: addOrbitMeasure
Let $H$ and $X$ be sets. Let $\nu$ be a measure of $H$. Let $\mu$ be a measure of $X$. Then $\addOrbitMeasureMACRoDIG {\nu}{\mu}$ is a measure of $X$ defined as $\mathrm{MeasureTheory\_\_Measure\_\_withDensity} (\mu , \mathrm{Classical\_\_epsilon} (\IsAddCutoffpartMACRoFTG {\nu}))$.
Definition 2.633src/Lib/General/Measure/SubgroupIntegral.lean: HasCutoff
Let $H$ and $X$ be sets. Let $\nu$ be a measure of $H$. Then $\HasCutoffMACRoAXD {\nu}{X}$ is a proposition defined as the proposition that there exists a function $\beta$ from $X$ to $\ENNRealMACRoCG$, such that $\IsCutoffMACRoBHC {\nu}{\beta}$.
The hypothesis that the action of H, with measure ν, on X admits a cutoff: a measurable weight with integral one over each orbit.
Definition 2.634src/Lib/General/Measure/SubgroupIntegral.lean: HasAddCutoff
Let $H$ and $X$ be sets. Let $\nu$ be a measure of $H$. Then $\HasAddCutoffMACRoAWS {\nu}{X}$ is a proposition defined as the proposition that there exists a function $\beta$ from $X$ to $\ENNRealMACRoCG$, such that $\IsAddCutoffMACRoBDZ {\nu}{\beta}$.

Definition 2.639src/Lib/General/Measure/SubgroupIntegral.lean: Subgroup.qms
Let $G$ be a set. Let $N$ be a subgroup of $G$. Let $\mu$ be an element of $\mathrm{autoParam}$ applied to the set of measures of $G$ and $\SubgroupqmsautoMACRoCZU$. Theorem 2.641src/Lib/General/Measure/SubgroupIntegral.lean: setIntegral_add_mem
Let $V$ be an additive commutative group. Let $X$ be a normed additive commutative group. Assume that $V$ is a measurable space. Let $\nu$ be a measure of $V$ such that $\nu$ is an additive left invariant. Let $M$ be a subgroup of $V$. Assume that $X$ is a normed space over $\Real$. Assume that $V$ is a topological space. Assume that $V$ is a topological additive group. Assume that $V$ is a Borel space. Let $F$ be a function from $V$ to $X$. Let $m \in V$ such that $m$ is an element of $M$. Then the Bochner integral of the restriction of $\nu$ and $M$ and the function that maps $x_{5}$ to $F (m + x_{5})$ is equal to the Bochner integral of the restriction of $\nu$ and $M$ and the function that maps $x_{6}$ to $F (x_{6})$.
Proof.
By 4 lemmas of Mathlib MeasureTheory.MeasurePreserving.setIntegral_image_emb, add_mem_cancel_left, MeasureTheory.measurePreserving_add_left, measurableEmbedding_addLeft
. ∎
Theorem 2.642src/Lib/General/Measure/SubgroupIntegral.lean: setIntegral_char
Let $V$ be an additive commutative group such that $V$ is a measurable space. Let $\nu$ be a measure of $V$ such that $\nu$ is an additive left invariant. Let $M$ be a subgroup of $V$. Assume that $V$ is a topological space. Assume that $V$ is a topological additive group. Assume that $V$ is a Borel space. Assume that $M$ is open. Let $\chi$ be a function from $V$ to $\ComplexMACRoAM$ such that for all elements $x_{5}$ and $y$ of $V$, $\chi (x_{5} + y)$ is equal to $\chi (x_{5})\times \chi (y)$. Then the Bochner integral of the restriction of $\nu$ and $M$ and the function that maps $x_{3}$ to $\chi (x_{3})$ is equal to $\iteMACRoDD {\mathrm{forall} (V , \lambda x_{4} . \mathrm{if} (\MembershipmemMACRoDQ {M}{x_{4}}, \EqtMACRoDE {\chi (x_{4})}{1}))}{\ComplexofRealMACRoFDV {\mathrm{MeasureTheory\_\_Measure\_\_real} (\nu , M)}}{0}$.
Orthogonality of characters: let ν be a translation-invariant Borel measure on V, M an open subgroup, and χ(x+y) = χ(x)χ(y) for all x, y in V. Then ∫_M χ dν is ν(M) (read as 0 if infinite) if χ = 1 on M, and 0 otherwise.
Proof. By setIntegral_add_mem
and 4 lemmas of Mathlib MeasureTheory.integral_const_mul, IsOpen.measurableSet, mul_eq_right₀, MeasureTheory.setIntegral_congr_fun
. ∎
Theorem 2.643src/Lib/General/Measure/SubgroupIntegral.lean: setIntegral_eq_finsum
Let $V$ be an additive commutative group. Let $X$ be a normed additive commutative group. Assume that $V$ is a measurable space. Let $\nu$ be a measure of $V$ such that $\nu$ is an additive left invariant. Let $M$ and $L$ be subgroups of $V$. Assume that $X$ is a normed space over $\Real$. Assume that $V$ is a topological space. Assume that $V$ is a topological additive group. Assume that $V$ is a Borel space. Assume that $\mathrm{MeasureTheory\_\_IsFiniteMeasureOnCompacts} (\nu)$. Assume that $X$ is a complete space. Assume that $V$ is a space. Assume that $M$ is compact. Assume that $L$ is open. Let $F$ be a function from $V$ to $X$ such that for all elements $x_{11}$ of $\SubtypeSet {x_{17}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoDQ {M}{x_{17}})}$, for all elements $y$ of $\SubtypeSet {x_{16}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoDQ {M}{x_{16}})}$, if $\MembershipmemMACRoDQ {L}{\NegnegMACRoFV {x_{11}}+ y}$, then $F (y)$ is equal to $F (x_{11})$. Then the Bochner integral of the restriction of $\nu$ and $M$ and the function that maps $x_{5}$ to $F (x_{5})$ is equal to $\HSMulhSMulMACRoEI {\mathrm{MeasureTheory\_\_Measure\_\_real} (\nu , \InterinterMACRoET {L}{M})}{\mathrm{finsum} (\lambda q . F (\mathrm{Quotient\_\_out} (q)))}$.
Proof. By IsCompact.relIndex_ne_zero_add, setIntegral_add_mem
and 13 lemmas of Mathlib QuotientAddGroup.eq, MeasurableAdd.measurable_const_add, MeasureTheory.IntegrableOn.indicator, MeasureTheory.integral_finsetSum, Fintype.sum_congr, IsOpen.measurableSet, MeasureTheory.integrableOn_const, MeasureTheory.setIntegral_congr_fun, Finset.smul_sum, AddSubgroup.mem_addSubgroupOf, IsCompact.measure_ne_top, Finset.sum_congr, IsCompact.measurableSet
. ∎

Lib.General.NumberTheory.ProcyclicImage

Theorem 2.644src/Lib/General/NumberTheory/ProcyclicImage.lean: exists_procyclic_generator
Let $x \in \Natural$. Let $X$ be a group. Assume that $x$ is prime. Assume that $X$ is a topological space. Assume that $X$ is a compact space. Assume that $X$ is a continuous product. Let $\kappa \in \MonoidHomMACRoHP {X}{\UnitsMACRoEL {\PadicIntMACRoFDG {x}}}$ such that $\kappa$ is continuous and for all elements $g_{1}$ of $X$, $4$ divides $\UnitsvalMACRoFDS {\kappa (g_{1})}- 1$. Then there exists an element $\gamma$ of $X$, such that for all subgroups $S \geq \MonoidHomkerMACRoIF {\kappa}$ of $X$, if $\SubgroupIsOpenMACRoCZS {S}$, then if $\gamma$ is an element of $S$, then for all elements $g$ of $X$, $g$ is an element of $S$.
A continuous κ from a compact group to ℤ_ℓ^×, with values ≡ 1 mod 4 if ℓ = 2, has procyclic image: for some γ, every open subgroup containing γ and ker κ is the whole group.
Proof.
By 36 lemmas of Mathlib sq_eq_one_iff, PadicInt.norm_p_pow, Nat.card_pos, orderOf_units, Nat.totient_prime_pow, pow_succ, Continuous.pow, Set.exists_subtype_range_iff, IsClosed.mem_of_frequently_of_tendsto, Subgroup.isClosed_of_isOpen, Continuous.isClosedMap, ZMod.isCyclic_units_of_prime_pow, Filter.Frequently.mono, IsCyclic.exists_monoid_generator, Subgroup.sq_mem_of_index_two, PadicInt.norm_le_pow_iff_mem_span_pow, PadicInt.ker_toZModPow, Topology.IsOpenEmbedding.tendsto_nhds_iff, isCompact_univ, Nat.card_zpowers, IsOpen.mem_nhds, PadicInt.pow_p_dvd_int_iff, pow_mem, Filter.tendsto_add_atTop_iff_nat, Subgroup.comap_map_eq_self, squeeze_zero_norm, ZMod.orderOf_five, Units.val_eq_one, ClusterPt.frequently, LE.le.trans, Subgroup.card_mul_index, Subgroup.isCyclic_of_le, Units.isOpenEmbedding_val, em', Fact.out, tendsto_sub_nhds_zero_iff
. ∎

Lib.General.NumberTheory.ClassNumberBound

Theorem 2.645src/Lib/General/NumberTheory/ClassNumberBound.lean: classNumber_le
Let $F$ be a field such that $F$ is a number field. Then the class number of $F$ is less than or equal to the exponentiation of the product of $2 ^ {\ModulefinrankMACRoFDO {\Rational}{F}}$ and the absolute value of the absolute discriminant of $F$ to $\ModulefinrankMACRoFDO {\Rational}{F}+ 1$.
The class number of a number field F of degree n over ℚ is at most (2ⁿ |disc F|)^{n+1}.
Proof.
By 20 lemmas of Mathlib Eq.ge, LE.le.trans_eq, Nat.card_le_card_of_surjective, Ideal.absNorm_span_natCast, Real.two_le_pi, Real.sqrt_le_self_iff, Ideal.absNorm_ne_zero_iff, Eq.trans_le, NumberField.RingOfIntegers.rank, Ideal.comap_map_quotientMk, Nat.factorial_le_pow, Ideal.absNorm_pos_of_nonZeroDivisors, Nat.card_sigma, NumberField.exists_ideal_in_class_of_norm_le, Finset.sum_le_sum, NumberField.InfinitePlace.card_add_two_mul_card_eq_rank, IsDedekindDomain.exists_sup_span_eq, Ideal.span_singleton_absNorm_le, Ideal.span_insert, Nat.card_eq_fintype_card
. ∎

Lib.General.NumberTheory.ZeroSymmetry

Theorem 2.647src/Lib/General/NumberTheory/ZeroSymmetry.lean: prod_euler_factor_ne_zero
Let $N , M \in \Natural$. Let $\chi$ be a Dirichlet character modulo $N$. Let $s$ be a complex such that $\ComplexreMACRoFEF {s}\neq 0$. Then $\FinsetProd {\mathrm{Nat\_\_primeFactors} (M)}{p}{(1 - \chi (p)\times p ^ {\NegnegMACRoFV {s}})}\neq 0$.
For a Dirichlet character χ, a natural number M and Re s ≠ 0, the product of the Euler factors 1 − χ(p)p^{−s} over the primes p dividing M is not zero.
Proof.
By 7 lemmas of Mathlib Complex.cpow_neg, Nat.Prime.pos, Finset.prod_ne_zero_iff, Complex.norm_natCast_cpow_of_pos, MulChar.star_apply', Nat.Prime.ne_one, MulChar.inv_apply_eq_inv'
. ∎
Theorem 2.648src/Lib/General/NumberTheory/ZeroSymmetry.lean: exists_isPrimitive_changeLevel_eq
Let $N$ be a nonzero natural number. Let $\chi$ be a Dirichlet character modulo $N$. Then there exists a natural number $M$, such that there exists an element $f$ of $\divides {M}{N}$, such that there exists a Dirichlet character $\psi$ modulo $M$, such that $M$ is nonzero, $\psi$ is primitive and $\mathrm{DirichletCharacter\_\_changeLevel\_\_app} (f , \psi)$ is equal to $\chi$.
Every complex Dirichlet character χ mod N, with N ≥ 1, is induced by a primitive character ψ mod M for some divisor M ≥ 1 of N.
Proof.
By 3 lemmas of Mathlib DirichletCharacter.primitiveCharacter_isPrimitive, DirichletCharacter.changeLevel_primitiveCharacter, DirichletCharacter.conductor_ne_zero
. ∎
Theorem 2.649src/Lib/General/NumberTheory/ZeroSymmetry.lean: LFunction_one_sub_eq_zero
Let $N$ be a nonzero natural number. Let $\chi$ be a Dirichlet character modulo $N$. Let $s$ be a complex such that $\ComplexreMACRoFEF {s}\neq 0$, $s \neq 1$ and $\DirichletCharacterLFunctionMACRoFDA {\chi}{s}$ is equal to $0$. Then the Gamma factor of $\chi$ and $s$ is equal to $0$ or $\DirichletCharacterLFunctionMACRoFDA {\InvinvMACRoFW {\chi}}{(1 - s)}$ is equal to $0$.
Symmetry of the zeros: if L(s, χ) = 0, re s ≠ 0 and s ≠ 1, then either s is a pole of the gamma factor of χ (where Mathlib gives it the value 0), or L(1 − s, χ⁻¹) = 0.
Proof. By exists_isPrimitive_changeLevel_eq, prod_euler_factor_ne_zero
and 7 lemmas of Mathlib map_inv, DirichletCharacter.LFunction_changeLevel, DirichletCharacter.IsPrimitive.completedLFunction_one_sub, DirichletCharacter.LFunction_eq_completed_div_gammaFactor, Complex.ext_iff, DirichletCharacter.conductor_inv, DirichletCharacter.changeLevel_eq_cast_of_dvd
. ∎
